<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Good governance rules can improve stock price:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="4%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">A new survey by McKinsey &amp; Co., international management consultant, shows that institutional investors are prepared to pay an 18%
premium for good corporate governance.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="4%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<I>Wall Street Journal; </I>June 19, 2000</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">We believe the company has not taken any significant step to improve its corporate governance rules.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Based on the 3 years of strong support the company is asked to:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">(1)&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="90%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Adopt this proposal or</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">(2)&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="90%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Submit this proposal to a vote of shareholders under company sponsorship.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"> Vote yes for:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>A<SMALL>NNUAL </SMALL>
E<SMALL>LECTION OF ALL DIRECTORS</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Y<SMALL>ES ON </SMALL>
6</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Board of Directors&#146; Response</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Board of Directors has evaluated the changes suggested by this proposal on several occasions. This evaluation has included guidance from outside advisers, including
a consultant on corporate governance issues. The Board believes that its classified Board structure, which has been in place since it was approved by the shareholders in 1986, continues to be in the best interests of the Company and its
shareholders.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Under the Company&#146;s By-Laws, the Board is divided into three classes with directors elected to staggered three-year terms. Approximately one-third of the directors
stand for election each year, and the entire Board can be replaced in the course of three annual meetings, all held within approximately two years. An active, professional board benefits in many ways from classifying its directors. Most notable
among these benefits are increased stability, improved long-term planning and enhanced independence.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The three-year staggered terms are designed to provide stability and ensure that a majority of the Company&#146;s directors at any given time have prior experience as
directors of the Company. This ensures that the Board has solid knowledge of the Company&#146;s complex business and products, as well as its product strategy. Directors who have experience with the Company and knowledge about its business are a
valuable resource and are better positioned to make the fundamental decisions that are best for the Company and its shareholders. The Board observes that numerous well-respected U.S. corporations and institutional investors have classified
boards.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Board believes that electing directors to staggered three-year terms enhances long-term strategic planning. We believe that the Board continuity made possible by
the classified Board structure is essential to the proper oversight of a company like Boeing that has high-technology products and programs that require major investments to be made over long periods of time. A classified board is appropriate for
Boeing and ensures responsible, knowledgeable representation of the long-term interests of Boeing and its shareholders. The annual election of only one-third of the Board also helps to prevent abrupt changes in corporate policies, based on misplaced
short-term objectives that might result if the entire Board could be replaced in one year.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">We believe that electing directors to three-year, as opposed to one-year, terms also enhances the independence of non-management directors by providing them with a
longer assured term of office. The existence of three-year terms for directors also assists the Company in attracting director candidates who are willing to make a longer-term commitment to the Company.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Board also believes that a classified Board structure enhances the Board&#146;s ability to negotiate the best results for shareholders in a takeover situation. One
benefit of having a classified board is that it encourages a person seeking to obtain control of the Company to negotiate with the Board. At least two annual shareholders meetings generally will be required to effect a change in control of the
Board. This gives the incumbent directors the time and leverage necessary to evaluate the adequacy and fairness of any takeover proposal, negotiate on behalf of all shareholders and weigh
alternative methods of maximizing shareholder value for all shareholders. It is important to note, however, that although the classified Board is intended to cause a person seeking to obtain control of the Company to negotiate with the Board, the
existence of a classified board will not, in fact, prevent a person from accomplishing a hostile acquisition.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>The Board believes that the benefits of the current classified board structure do not come at the cost of directors&#146; accountability to shareholders. We believe
that directors elected to three-year terms are just as accountable to shareholders as directors elected annually, since all directors are required to uphold their fiduciary duties to the Company and its shareholders, regardless of the length of
their term of office. </B>The annual election of one-third of the directors provides shareholders with an orderly means to effect change and communicate their views on the performance of the Company and its directors.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="2">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Approval of the proposal would not automatically eliminate the classified board, as this proposal is only a recommendation. Eliminating the classified board would
require the affirmative vote of at least 75% of the outstanding shares on a proposal to amend Article II, Section 1 of the Company&#146;s By-Laws, which provides for a classified board.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>T<SMALL>HE </SMALL>
B<SMALL>OARD OF </SMALL>
D<SMALL>IRECTORS </SMALL>
U<SMALL>NANIMOUSLY </SMALL>
R<SMALL>ECOMMENDS</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B><SMALL>A </SMALL>
V<SMALL>OTE </SMALL>
AGAINST P<SMALL>ROPOSAL </SMALL>
6.</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"> <B>
<A NAME="753tx46"></A>
PROPOSAL 7</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>SHAREHOLDER PROPOSAL ON PENSION PLANS</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Two shareholders have advised the Company that they intend to present the following resolution at the Annual Meeting. In accordance with applicable proxy regulations,
the proposed resolution and supporting statement, for which the Board of Directors and the Company accept no responsibility, are set forth below. Approval of this proposal would require the affirmative vote of a majority of the outstanding shares of
Boeing stock present in person or by proxy and entitled to vote at the Annual Meeting.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Shareholder Resolution</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" WIDTH="95%" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">RESOLVED: the shareholders request that the Boeing Board of Directors adopt the following policy:</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="10%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">(1)&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="85%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">All non-represented employees be given a choice between the old Boeing [Heritage] pension plans used prior to Jan 1, 1999, or
the current Pension Value cash-balance plan at time of termination or retirement.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="10%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">(2)&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="85%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The cash balance plan to provide a monthly annuity at least equal to that expected under the old pension plan, or an
actuarially equivalent lump sum.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Proponents&#146; Supporting Statement</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Boeing announced in 1998, and implemented in 1999, a new pension plan for over 100,000 non represented employees. None were given a choice of old or new plans. The IRS
has yet to determine the tax status of the Boeing Pension Value cash-balance plan. Without tax qualified status, participants can be taxed on the value of the benefit they earn under a company-sponsored retirement plan each year. The company also
risks significant tax-liabilities.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The legality of cash-balance pension plans has been challenged in courts, are currently under government scrutiny, and have been the subject of extensive press
coverage. In many cases, older workers have found out they now must work years longer to get the same benefits promised under the old plans. The EEOC has created a task force to gather information and complaints from employees relating to
age-discrimination issues. Congressional hearings have been held, and related legislation has been promoted by corporate lobbyists.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Boeing offered the same plan to the Society of Professional Engineering Employees in Aerospace (SPEEA). The cash-balance plan and the company offered medical insurance
takeaways were a factor in the largest white collar strike in history. The company has never offered a reason for no choice of plans for current employees.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Some older employees must now work extra years to reach the same pension previously promised under the Heritage plans. To change the goal-posts for employees nearing
retirement by using methods of questionable legality which have been the subject of government investigations for over a year is inappropriate for a company that wants to keep its best employees.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Cash-balance plans have incited huge protest meetings, union organizing, adverse media coverage, and Senate hearings. For example, an IBM shareholder proposal making a
similar request last year
got a shareholder vote of 28.4%, and support from organizations like CALPERS. (California Public Employees Retirement System)</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Please encourage the BOD to keep their previous pension promises by giving an informed choice to loyal employees who have helped make the company famous and profitable.
Thank you.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Board of Directors&#146; Response</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Boeing Company designed its new pension plan, the Pension Value Plan or PVP, to provide a single plan for the Company&#146;s non-represented salaried employees.
Before the PVP was implemented in 1999, these employees earned benefits under more than 20 different plans and formulas that were sponsored by the &#147;premerger&#148; companies: Boeing, McDonnell Douglas and Rockwell. The PVP was designed to
provide a level of benefits that would be nearly equivalent to the benefits provided under the prior plans. The Company did not adopt the PVP to reduce pension-related costs. On the contrary, the PVP increased the Company&#146;s pension liability
substantially, as was disclosed in the 1998 Annual Report.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The PVP is not subject to the same criticisms as other cash-balance plans because it includes important features that are different from most other cash-balance pension
plans:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE BORDER="0" WIDTH="100%" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="2%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"></FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="Wingdings">&#159;</FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000"
FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="98%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The PVP preserves all benefits earned under the former plans, and allows these benefits to continue growing in proportion to
the employee&#146;s salary.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE BORDER="0" WIDTH="100%" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="2%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"></FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="Wingdings">&#159;</FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000"
FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="98%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Employees began earning new benefits under the PVP formula immediately upon the PVP&#146;s implementation rather than having
a &#147;wear-away&#148; transition period before they could accrue any new benefits under the cash-balance plan.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE BORDER="0" WIDTH="100%" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="2%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"></FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="Wingdings">&#159;</FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000"
FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="98%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The PVP increases the percentage of pay that is credited to the employee&#146;s cash-balance &#147;account&#148; as the
employee&#146;s age increases. Thus, the PVP gives the Company&#146;s oldest employees nearly four times more benefit credits each year than their youngest counterparts receive.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Company believes, based upon its analysis, that for most employees who were near retirement age or who had long service when the PVP took effect, there is little
difference in projected retirement benefits. In fact, due to the PVP&#146;s unusual features and the extra costs associated with this change, the Company further believes (based again on its analysis) that many employees&#146; projected PVP benefits
are slightly higher than the projected benefits from their former plans. Of course, different employees will be impacted differently. Contrary to the Proponent&#146;s Supporting Statement, overall, the PVP provides a level of benefits that is very
close to, and in some cases better than, the benefits provided by the prior plans.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="2">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Proponent&#146;s Supporting Statement alleges that the Company has broken &#147;previous pension promises&#148; made to employees. As just discussed, the Company
does not believe that any promise was broken. All benefits that employees had already accrued under their prior plans have been preserved, as required by federal pension law. Both federal pension law and the terms of the prior pension plans gave the
Company the right to change its pension plans for the future, as long as it did not reduce the benefits employees had already accrued. Boeing exceeded its legal obligation to merely preserve accrued benefits by not only protecting all of the prior
plans&#146; accrued benefits, but providing for the future growth of those benefits in proportion to employees&#146; future salary growth, instead of merely freezing the prior accrued benefits.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="2">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"> The proposal&#146;s recommendation to permit employees to choose between the PVP and their former plan upon termination or retirement would undermine a primary purpose
of the PVP, to create a single, simplified plan for all salaried non-represented employees. Employers are not required to offer their employees a choice between pension plans, and most employers do not, for good business reasons. Large costs and
significant administrative difficulties would be associated with maintaining numerous plans and benefit formulas, meeting the myriad federal regulations that apply to each plan, and offering and implementing employees&#146; choices as they terminate
or retire over a period of decades.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">In summary, Boeing strives to provide its employees with a total compensation and benefits package that is competitive and that helps the Company attract and retain the
best performers. Management believes that the PVP meets these criteria, and that the PVP&#146;s generous, carefully thought-out transition measures have protected the transition-date workforce. Boeing will continually review its benefit plans and
programs, making changes where appropriate. At this time, the Board is satisfied that the measures recommended by the proposal would be unnecessarily detrimental to the Company&#146;s pension and compensation programs.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Proponent&#146;s Supporting Statement</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">We believe this proposal addresses the considerable attention currently devoted to corporate audit committees, flawed financial statements and dubious accounting
treatments. Chairman Arthur Levitt of the Securities and Exchange Commission has continuing concerns about deficient year-end audits and aggressive accounting practices that enable companies to manage earnings and meet investment community
expectations.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Responding to Chairman Levitt&#146;s concerns, the New York Stock Exchange and the National Association of Securities Dealers established The Blue Ribbon Committee on
Improving the Effectiveness of Corporate Audit Committees. The Committee made a formal report that highlighted the importance of company audit committee members&#146; independence and baseline financial literacy.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">It is particularly important for Boeing to excel in an effective review of its cost accounting and audit procedures.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Vote yes for:</FONT></DIV>

<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>S<SMALL>HAREHOLDER </SMALL>
V<SMALL>OTE ON </SMALL>
A<SMALL>UDIT </SMALL>

</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Y<SMALL>ES ON </SMALL>
8</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"> <B>Board of Directors&#146; Response</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>This proposal is unnecessary because the Company already complies with the new Securities and Exchange Commission and New York Stock Exchange
requirements.</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">In response to the Blue Ribbon Committee mentioned above, the NYSE adopted new rules for audit committees of listed companies. The rules require, first, that the Board
of Directors adopt and approve a formal written charter for the audit committee that contains specified statements as to the committee&#146;s responsibilities.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Second, the NYSE rules require each company to maintain an audit committee that &#147;shall consist of at least three directors, all of whom have no relationship to the
company that may interfere with the exercise of their independence from management and the company&#148; in addition to other criteria of independence.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Third, the new NYSE rules also require all audit committee members to be &#147;financially literate&#148; in the business judgment of the Board, or become so within a
reasonable time after appointment.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The SEC also adopted new rules, which require</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TR>
<TD WIDTH="2%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"></FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="Wingdings">&#159;</FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000"
FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="98%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">a report of the audit committee to be included in a proxy statement once each year if directors are to be
elected,</FONT></DIV>
</TD>

statement as Appendix A. The Report of the Audit Committee, confirming compliance with these rules, begins on page 13.</FONT></DIV>

thoroughly studied and widely discussed. New rules have been adopted by the responsible regulatory bodies and the Company complies with them. This proposal is not needed.</FONT></DIV>

<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Shareholder Resolution</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">RESOLVED: Limit Stock Dilution.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Boeing shareholders recommend that the company not present any stock option
proposal to shareholders that could make Boeing total stock option dilution greater than Boeing&#146;s industry peer group. Boeing&#146;s peer group is based on standard industrial code groups.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Proponent&#146;s Supporting Statement</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Management recommended shareholder approval for a stock option plan in its Year 2000 proxy that could dilute Boeing stock 37% higher than the level recommended by
Institutional Shareholder Services. (Source: ISS Proxy Analysis, Boeing Company, April 21, 2000). The 37% higher dilution is based on a Boeing stock dilution comparison with Boeing&#146;s industry peers.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The 37% higher dilution calculation is based on the cost as top executives and others exercise options at fixed prices&#151;below current market prices. The calculation
is a comparison between Boeing and the top quartile of Boeing&#146;s industry peers.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Furthermore, the Year 2000 proposal to add shares to the stock option plan:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">(1)&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
<TD WIDTH="90%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Benefits consultants, agents, advisors and independent contractors in addition to senior executives.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>

<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The public perception of the company stock option plan is:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Boeing executives are to reap riches. Boeing gave 2,000 senior executives $140 million in stock last week under an incentive plan
triggered by the company&#146;s recent stock price.</FONT></DIV>

to get about $23 million, according to company regulatory filings.</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>

<TABLE WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<I>Seattle Times</I>, Oct. 3, 2000</FONT></DIV>
</TD>
</TR>
</TABLE>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"> Meanwhile, the shareholder lawsuit filed in Seattle Federal Court, that Boeing executives allegedly concealed production problems in an attempt to preserve the
company&#146;s stock price, remains unresolved.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">To maintain and increase shareholder value, vote yes to:</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>L<SMALL>IMIT </SMALL>
S<SMALL>TOCK </SMALL>
D<SMALL>ILUTION</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Y<SMALL>ES ON </SMALL>
9</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Board of Directors&#146; Response</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Board of Directors recommends a vote AGAINST this proposal because it fails to adequately define essential terms and would therefore be impossible to implement.
Moreover, the Board believes that any attempt by the Company to implement the proposal, based on the Company&#146;s best efforts to decipher the proponent&#146;s intent, could impede the Company&#146;s ability to maintain appropriate equity
incentive compensation programs for its executives. The proposal would also disenfranchise current and future shareholders by prohibiting the Company from presenting executive compensation programs to shareholders for their approval if certain
ill-defined dilution standards were exceeded.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The proposal would forbid the Board from presenting &#147;any stock option proposal to shareholders that could make Boeing total stock option dilution greater than
Boeing&#146;s industry peer group.&#148; The proponent does not identify the companies he believes would constitute the Company&#146;s peer group, but merely states that &#147;Boeing&#146;s peer group is based on standard industrial code
groups.&#148; Standard industrial code groups (&#147;SIC groups&#148;) are used by governmental agencies for reporting purposes and to compile and produce data with respect to the national economy and its sectors. Many companies, including Boeing,
report under numerous different SIC groups. Moreover, of the SIC groups Boeing reports under, the population of any one group is not necessarily the same as the next.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Even if the proponent had identified one or more specific SIC groups, the companies that make up the group would not constitute the Company&#146;s &#147;peers,&#148; as
that term is commonly understood, since SIC groups do not take into consideration the capitalization, size, revenues, or profits of the companies in the groups. For purposes of comparing the dilutive effects of a stock option-related proposal on its
stock option pool, Boeing should not be required to consider as peers all companies included in a SIC group.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">In addition to these concerns, the proposal presents several other barriers to implementation. It provides no guidance as to the point in time the percentage dilution
is to be measured, or what the Company would be required to do if for reasons beyond its control its percentage dilution came to exceed that of the other companies in the SIC groups. The Board cannot foretell what, if any, increases in the Plan may
be more dilutive than its peers&#146; plans, since the Board has no control over how other companies manage their plans. Also, the dilutive effects of a stock option-related proposal may be more dilutive than those of one of the SIC groups Boeing
reports under, but not another.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Aside from the mechanical difficulties inherent in the proposal, its implementation would unnecessarily undermine the Board&#146;s flexibility in maintaining the
Company&#146;s compensation programs and its ability to submit changes in those programs for shareholder consideration. Last
year, the Board submitted a proposal to shareholders to amend the 1997 Incentive Stock Plan to increase the number of authorized shares. The Board stated its belief that adoption of the proposal would, among other things, enhance the long-term
shareholder value of the Company by offering opportunities to the Company&#146;s employees, officers, consultants, agents, and advisors to acquire a proprietary interest in the Company and to link their interests and efforts to the long-term
interests of the Company&#146;s shareholders. Over 81 percent of the shares present and eligible to vote at the 2000 Annual Meeting approved the proposal.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The proponent claims that the 1997 Incentive Stock Plan presented at last year&#146;s annual meeting &#147;could dilute Boeing stock 37% higher than the level
recommended by Institutional Shareholder Services,&#148; citing an April 2000 &#147;ISS Proxy Analysis.&#148; This simply is not true. There is nothing in the ISS Proxy Analysis that speaks of such 37% higher dilution. Furthermore, the Company
believes that the proponent has misstated the method by which ISS analyzed the level of dilution.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Board believes that the overwhelming support manifested for last year&#146;s proposal was a strong and positive endorsement of the Company&#146;s compensation
program objectives and goals. The goals of the compensation program are discussed in detail in the Compensation Committee Report on page 29 of the Proxy Statement. The Company&#146;s ability to continue to meet these goals is dependent on continued
flexibility and sound management of the various components of its compensation program. This includes periodic reevaluation of the number of shares available under its compensation plans.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">To continue its market leadership, the Company must provide compensation and incentive programs that will retain and continue to attract the best employees.
Accordingly, the Board must be permitted to effectively manage those programs. When circumstances require, the Board must also be able to submit issues related to those programs for shareholder consideration. The Board believes that the proposal
would impede the Company&#146;s ability to maintain appropriate equity incentive compensation programs for its employees.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>T<SMALL>HE </SMALL>
B<SMALL>OARD OF </SMALL>
D<SMALL>IRECTORS </SMALL>
U<SMALL>NANIMOUSLY </SMALL>
R<SMALL>ECOMMENDS</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B><SMALL>A </SMALL>
V<SMALL>OTE </SMALL>
AGAINST P<SMALL>ROPOSAL </SMALL>
9.</B></FONT></DIV>

for such information. Requests may be sent to the Corporate Secretary, The Boeing Company, P.O. Box 3707, Mail Code 13-08, Seattle, Washington 98124-2207, or submitted by calling (206) 544-1056.</FONT></DIV>

APPENDIX A</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="5">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>CHARTER OF THE AUDIT COMMITTEE</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>OF THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS OF</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>THE BOEING COMPANY</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>(as amended May 1, 2000)</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>O<SMALL>RGANIZATION</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Members:</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The Audit Committee shall consist of three or more directors who are not members of the management and meet the independence and expertise requirements as defined by
the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) Listed Company Manual. The chairperson and members of the Committee shall be appointed by the Board of Directors at the annual organization meeting of the Board.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>Meetings:</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Audit Committee meetings shall be in conjunction with regular Board of Directors meetings and at such other times as called by or on behalf of the chairperson of the
Committee.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">A majority of the members of the Audit Committee shall constitute a quorum. The Committee shall act only on the affirmative vote of at least two of the
members.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="3">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>R<SMALL>OLES</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The function of the Audit Committee is oversight. In fulfilling their responsibilities hereunder, it is recognized that the members of the Audit Committee are not
full-time employees of the Company and are not, and do not represent themselves to be, accountants or auditors by profession. As such, it is not the duty of the Audit Committee or its members to conduct &#147;fieldwork&#148; or other types of
auditing or accounting reviews or procedures, and each member of the Audit Committee shall be entitled to rely on (i) the judgement of those persons and organizations within and outside the Company that it receives information from and (ii) the
accuracy of the financial and other information provided to the Audit Committee by such persons or organizations.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>R<SMALL>ESPONSIBILITIES</SMALL>
</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The responsibilities of the Audit Committee are to:</FONT></DIV>

recommend changes to the Internal Audit Charter.</FONT></DIV>

<TR>
<TD WIDTH="6%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"></FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="Wingdings">&#159;</FONT><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000"

<P>&nbsp;</P>
<P ALIGN="center">ELECTION TO OBTAIN FUTURE MATERIALS
<BR>
OF THE BOEING COMPANY
<BR>
ELECTRONICALLY INSTEAD OF BY MAIL</P>
<P ALIGN="left">Boeing shareholders may elect to receive future materials through the Internet <U>instead</U> of receiving copies through the mail. Boeing is offering this service to provide added convenience to its shareholders and to reduce
printing and mailing costs.</P>
<P ALIGN="left">To take advantage of this option, shareholders must subscribe to one of the various commerical services that offer access to the Internet World Wide Web. Costs normally associated with electronic access, such as usage and telephone
charges, will be borne by the shareholder.</P>
<P ALIGN="left"><B>To elect this option, go to the website http://www.econsent.com/ba/</B>. You will be asked to enter the 9 digit Account Number located in the second group of numbers appearing beneath the perforation line on the reverse side.
Shareholders who elect this option will be notified each year by e-mail how to access the proxy materials and how to vote their shares on the Internet.</P>
<P ALIGN="left">If you consent to receive the Company's future materials electronically, your consent will remain in effect unless it is withdrawn by calling, writing, or e-mailing our Transfer Agent, Fleet (EquiServe), at 1-888-777-0923; P.O. Box
8038, Boston, MA 02266-8038; www.equiserve.com. Also, if while this consent is in effect you decide you would like to receive a hard copy of the proxy materials, you may call, write, or e-mail our Transfer Agent.</P>
<P ALIGN="center">You may access The Boeing Company</P>
<P ALIGN="center">annual report and proxy statement at:</P>
<P ALIGN="center">www.boeing.com/proxy</P>

<P ALIGN="center"><B>SOLICITED BY THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS</B></P>
<P ALIGN="center"><B>THE BOEING COMPANY</B></P>
<P ALIGN="center"><B>ANNUAL MEETING OF
<BR>
SHAREHOLDERS
<BR>
APRIL 30, 2001</B></P>
<P>The undersigned hereby appoints Philip M. Condit, Kenneth M. Duberstein and Paul E. Gray (the &quot;Proxy Committee&quot;), and each of them, with the power of substitution, proxies for the undersigned and authorizes them to represent and vote
all of the shares of stock of the Company which the undersigned may be entitled to vote at the Annual Meeting of Shareholders to be held on April 30, 2001 (the &quot;Meeting&quot;), and at any adjournment thereof, as indicated on the reverse side of
this card with respect to Proposals 1 through 9, and with discretionary authority as to any other matters that may properly come before the Meeting, in accordance with and as described in the Notice and Proxy Statement for the Meeting.</P>
<P>If there are shares of stock allocated to the undersigned in any of the retirement, savings and employee stock ownership plans listed in the Proxy Statement under the heading &quot;Voting by Participants in Employee Plans,&quot; the undersigned
hereby instructs the trustee of each such plan to vote all of such shares at the Meeting and any adjournment thereof, as indicated on the reverse side of this card with respect to Proposals 1 through 9, and authorizes the trustee to vote in its
judgment or to empower the Proxy Committee to vote in the Proxy Committee's judgment, on such other business as may properly come before the Meeting and any adjournment thereof.</P>
<P>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>If no direction is given, this proxy will be voted FOR Proposal 1 and AGAINST Proposals 2 through 9.</B></P>

<DIV ALIGN="center"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times"><B>2001 ANNUAL MEETING OF SHAREHOLDERS</B></FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">The location of the 2001 Annual Meeting of Shareholders has been changed. The Company&#146;s Building 2-22, in which we planned to hold the Annual Meeting, was damaged
in the earthquake that struck the Seattle area on February 28, 2001. After a thorough evaluation of the structure, it has been determined that it may not be possible to complete any necessary repairs in time for the Annual Meeting. The date of the
Annual Meeting remains the same, but it has been moved to The Westin Seattle, 1900 Fifth Avenue, Seattle, Washington. A revised Notice of Annual Meeting of Shareholders appears on the reverse side of this page.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="2">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">For directions to The Westin Seattle, visit www.westin.com or call (206) 728-1000.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="2">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Parking is available, at the shareholder&#146;s expense, in The Westin Seattle&#146;s garage and at other lots in the vicinity.</FONT></DIV>
<DIV><FONT SIZE="2">&nbsp;</FONT></DIV>
<DIV ALIGN="left"><FONT SIZE="3" COLOR="#000000" FACE="'Times New Roman', Times">Information on public transportation may be obtained from METRO by calling (206) 553-3000.</FONT></DIV>
